OpenDefocus

Welcome



Logo thanks to Welmoed Boersma!

An advanced open-source convolution library for image post-processing

Donate • Features • User guide • Browser viewer • Download • Installation • Issues

New to OpenDefocus?

The best way to start is by reading the Quickstart. This user targeted guide contains everything you need to know to get the most out of OpenDefocus.

Looking for specific features? Look at the detailed section which describes all the features more thouroughly.

User features

  • Entirely free! (but please consider donating!)
  • Native integration for camera data to match convolution to real world camera data.
  • GPU accelerated (Vulkan/Metal)
  • Both simple 2D defocus as well as depth based (1/Z, real or direct math based)
  • Fast and Accurate quality modes: Fast for quick previews, Accurate for exact final renders.
  • Lots of non uniform artifacts:
    • Catseye
    • Barndoors
    • Astigmatism
    • Axial aberration
    • Inversed bokehs in foreground
  • Easy to use bokeh creator or use your own image

Technical features

  • Process each pixel coordinate and channel on the image with a custom filter kernel. For a simple RGBA 1920x1080 image, that is at least 8.294.400 custom kernels!
  • Written in Rust (stable channel) with WGSL shaders run through WGPU; no Protobuf, C++ or CMake toolchain is needed.
  • One GPU renderer for both quality modes, distributed as an OpenFX plugin, a CLI and a viewer.
  • Easy to use and open API to hook into your own application or DCC.
  • Lots of control over the output, take a look at all options available.

Donate

OpenDefocus is free and open source. To ask about supporting development, contact the maintainers through the OpenDefocus project.

You can also help by reporting reproducible bugs, sharing examples, improving the documentation, or contributing code.

v0.2.2

Fixed

  • (ci) use the Natron Windows archive and make a test type unambiguous on Windows

v0.2.1

Fixed

  • (render) make the far occlusion and the front hand-over continuous
  • (render) blend a nearly fully hidden far gather to the far surface colour
  • (render) hide the far background behind less blurred far surfaces
  • (render) remove the sharp line and ticks at the focal plane

Other

  • (render) tidy far occlusion and Fast composite code

v0.2.0

Added

  • add Nuke version to 12 and higher

Fixed

  • build opendefocus without the wgpu feature
  • (shader) avoid pointers to struct members so the SPIR-V writer accepts Accurate
  • sample points are now determined circular to prevent unnecessary calculations and fix the simple shape to be circular
  • Change donate url

Changed

  • Breaking: the Nuke plugin is replaced by an OpenFX plugin (opendefocus-ofx). Rendering now goes through a native WGPU renderer (Vulkan/Metal) instead of the previous kernel crates; there is no CPU or legacy fallback.
  • Quality has two modes: Fast (renders at half resolution with sampled gathers and exact discs for bright highlights with a plain aperture, about 3x faster than the old Low and 15x or more faster than Accurate on 1080p frames, within roughly 50 dB PSNR of Accurate on the test scenes) and Accurate (exact, default). Low, Medium, High, Custom and the Samples parameter are removed.
  • The user guide is rewritten for the OpenFX plugin.

Added

  • OpenFX plugin with tiled rendering and an optional camera input.
  • opendefocus-cli, a command line renderer, and opendefocus-ui, a viewer.
  • Linux and Windows OFX installers.
  • circle-of-confusion, bokeh-creator and image-ndarray crates inside the workspace.
  • cargo xtask automation for building, testing, benchmarking and packaging, plus CI for tests, Natron host rendering, PR render previews and releases.
  • Contributing guide and architecture notes.

Removed

  • The opendefocus-nuke, opendefocus-kernel, opendefocus-shared, opendefocus-datastructure and spirv-cli-build crates.

Fixed

  • Building opendefocus without the wgpu feature.
  • Accurate shaders failing SPIR-V validation because of pointers to struct members.

Installation

Download the installer for your system from the latest release on GitHub, then restart your host. A host that is already running does not see a new plugin.

SystemDownloadInstalls to
LinuxOpenDefocus-OFX-<version>-linux-<arch>.tar.gz/usr/OFX/Plugins
WindowsOpenDefocus-OFX-<version>-windows-x86_64-setup.exe%CommonProgramFiles%\OFX\Plugins

<arch> is x86_64 or aarch64 on Linux.

Linux: extract the archive and run ./install.sh (use --prefix DIR for another location). The folder also contains uninstall.sh.

Windows: run the setup .exe. The installer is not signed yet, so Windows SmartScreen shows “Windows protected your PC”. Click More info, then Run anyway. Uninstall it from Settings > Apps.

Releases are published for Linux and Windows. On macOS, build the plugin from source with the building guide.

Each download has a matching .sha256 file to verify it.

Important

The plugin renders on the GPU (through WGPU). The machine needs a GPU, or on Linux a software Vulkan driver such as Mesa lavapipe.

See Natron and Resolve for using the plugin in a host. To build from source, including the CLI and viewer, see the building guide.

Natron and DaVinci Resolve

OpenDefocus runs as an OpenFX plugin, so any host that loads OpenFX can use it. This page shows how to install it and use it in Natron and DaVinci Resolve.

Natron DaVinci Resolve
Best forCompositing with a separate Depth inputGrading and editing: the effect on a clip
Defocus modes2D and Depth2D (Depth needs a second input, see below)
Where it appearsNode menu, OpenDefocusEffects, Open FX > OpenDefocus
Checked onNatron 2.6 alpha, macOS arm64Resolve Free 21.1.1, macOS arm64

Note

Only these combinations have been run. Linux, Windows, Resolve Studio and other versions should work, but are untested. Nuke Non-Commercial does not load OpenFX plugins.

Install

Install OpenDefocus from the latest release, as described in Installation, then restart the host. A host that is already running does not see a new plugin.

To install by hand, copy OpenDefocus.ofx.bundle into the OpenFX folder of your system:

SystemFolder
macOS/Library/OFX/Plugins
Windows%CommonProgramFiles%\OFX\Plugins
Linux/usr/OFX/Plugins

Important

The plugin renders on the GPU (through WGPU). The machine needs a GPU, or on Linux a software Vulkan driver such as Mesa lavapipe.

Natron

OpenDefocus appears in the node menu under OpenDefocus. To try it without installing system-wide, start Natron with the folder that contains the bundle:

OFX_PLUGIN_PATH=/path/to/folder-with-bundle /path/to/Natron

Depth defocus in four steps

  1. Create the node and connect your image to Source (input 0).
  2. Connect the depth map to Depth (input 1).
  3. On the Defocus tab set Defocus Mode to Depth.
  4. Set Math to what your depth map contains, and Focal Plane to the value that should stay sharp. See Depth math and Focal plane.

The screenshot uses the bundled occlusion scene: a depth map with Math set to Direct, where each depth value is already a blur size in pixels.

From Python:

node = app1.createNode("org.opendefocus.OpenDefocus")
node.connectInput(0, source)
node.connectInput(1, depth)
node.getParam("defocusMode").setValue(1)   # 1 = Depth

When you write the result, set the Write node to 32-bit float. The default is half float.

DaVinci Resolve

After a restart, the effect is in the Effects library under Open FX > OpenDefocus. Drag it onto a clip on the Edit or Color page.

  1. Drop OpenDefocus on the clip (or on a node).
  2. Open the clip’s Inspector > Effects > Open FX tab. The parameters are the same as everywhere else, in four groups: Defocus, Camera, Bokeh and Non-Uniform.
  3. Set Size and Max Size. In the default 2D mode the whole image is blurred by that size, and no depth map is needed.
  4. Turn on Non-Uniform effects such as Catseye when you want a lens look. They centre on the middle of the frame.

Tip

Resolve shows the generic effect icon for OpenDefocus. Resolve refuses the plugin’s icon property, so the icon cannot be changed from the plugin.

Depth in Resolve

On the Edit and Color pages an effect only gets the clip it sits on, so there is no place to connect a depth map. Use 2D mode there.

Depth mode needs a second input. Whether and where Resolve offers one for OpenFX (for example in the Fusion page) has not been tested with OpenDefocus. If you try it, connect the depth map to the second input and follow the same four depth steps as in Natron.

Speed

Quality sets how exactly the blur is integrated:

QualityUse it for
FastEditing, scrubbing and playback. Quick preview-grade approximation.
Accurate (default)Final renders. Exact integration, and the slowest.

Warning

DaVinci Resolve renders Accurate noticeably slower than other hosts. Use Fast while you edit and switch to Accurate for the final render.

Large Size values and large frames cost more. If playback is heavy, drop to Fast while you work and switch to Accurate for the final render.

Troubleshooting

  • The plugin does not show up. Restart the host after copying the bundle, and check the folder in the table above.
  • “Rendering Failed” (Natron) or a black frame. The reason is in the host’s log. A common one is Depth mode with nothing connected to Depth: the log says “Depth clip is not connected”.
  • Natron layers. Only RGBA is processed. Layer selectors are ignored.
  • Camera input. The optional Camera input only exists in hosts that provide the Nuke camera extension. In Natron and Resolve use the parameters on the Camera tab.
  • Big frames. 4K frames can use around 2 GB of memory with Astigmatism on.

Welcome

This is the user guide for OpenDefocus. For a quick summary go to the quickstart. For details about features (non-technical) go to the detailed page.

  • Quickstart
  • Detailed

Quickstart

This is a quick reference guide to getting started defocusing with the OpenDefocus OpenFX plugin. It links to more detailed descriptions, but this should get you far enough to make some pretty images!

Each parameter has a hint, shown as a tooltip in the host. If you ever need some information, just hover over the parameter.

If you’re looking for explanations, the Detailed page contains that. If you’re searching something but don’t know where to find it? The search bar can probably find it for you.

The parameters are grouped in four tabs: Defocus, Camera, Bokeh and Non-Uniform. We’ll go through them in that order.

Index

  • Inputs
  • Options
  • Quality
  • Bokeh
  • Non uniform

Inputs

The plugin has two inputs, and a third optional input in hosts that provide a camera.

  1. Source: the image you want to defocus. Only RGBA is processed; the plugin does not select layers or channels. The Source is assumed to be premultiplied.
  2. Depth: the depth map used in Depth mode. It can be RGBA or alpha, and the first channel is used. It must be connected in Depth mode and is not used in 2D mode.
  3. Camera (optional): only shown in hosts that provide the Nuke camera extension. A connected camera supplies the focal length and filmback. See Camera Input.

The output is float RGBA, premultiplied. Errors, such as a missing Depth input in Depth mode, are written to the host’s log.

Options

The Defocus tab is responsible for the defocus settings: how the defocus is applied, its size, and the focal plane.

The most important parameters are:

  • Defocus Mode: 2D (the defocus is set by Size only) or Depth (the defocus is computed from the Depth input).
  • Math: only in Depth mode, defines the type of depth map being used. Either Real or 1/Z (default). ( Depth math)
  • Size: the size of the defocus in pixels (default 5).
  • Max Size: in Depth mode, the maximum defocus value (default 10). ( Defocus size)
  • Focal Plane: the depth value that stays in focus (default 0). ( Focal plane)
  • Quality: Fast or Accurate (default). ( Quality)

The Defocus tab also has Protect (the width of the in-focus band around the focal plane), Size Multiplier and Gamma.

Quality

This is a feature that makes it possible for OpenDefocus to render complicated shapes but having a fast preview.

Tip

Large bokehs and lots of motion show the difference between Fast and Accurate most. Check them in Accurate before the final render.

The Quality parameter (Defocus tab) has two choices: Fast and Accurate. The default is Accurate.

  • Fast is a quick approximation of Accurate with the same features. It renders at half resolution, upsamples the result and keeps pixels the blur does not change at full resolution. Expect slightly softer or noisier bokeh edges; in-focus areas stay sharp.
  • Accurate is exact and slower.

It is recommended to set the quality to Fast during compositing work, and to Accurate for the final render.

Quality settings: Fast (first), Accurate (second) and their difference, amplified 8 times (third).

Bokeh

Bokeh tab

OpenDefocus includes an easy to use and extended bokeh tab. Allowing you to create any bokeh you’d like.

You can find the settings under the tab Bokeh.

Filter type

The Filter Type parameter defines the shape creation. The plugin offers three choices:

  • Simple (default): the fast built-in filter. It ignores the other Bokeh settings, which are greyed out.
  • Disc: a round bokeh shape.
  • Blade: a polygon shape similar to the f-stop blades.

Blade

When Blade is selected, the number of blades is set by the Blades parameter (3 to 16 on the slider, default 5). Blades is greyed out for Disc and Simple. The sharpness is defined by the Curvature parameter (default 0.5). Setting a value to 1 will make it entirely circular. Setting the value to 0 will make the corners sharp.

Curvature comparison, curvature 0 and curvature 1

Under Disc and Blade, the other Bokeh parameters (Radius, Ring Color, Inner Color, Ring Size, Outer Blur, Inner Blur, Angle, Aspect Ratio and the Noise parameters) shape the result.

Non uniform

The Non-Uniform tab is responsible for any effects that are unique depending on their depth and position on screen. This can be a cutting, cropping, scaling, etc. effect.

This is where it gets really fun. There are now more effects than ever, including effects that were technically not possible at all before.

For a detailed technical explanations about these effects, like how they occur physically, I’d refer you to the Non uniform page.

Each effect has its own group with an Enable checkbox. Its other parameters are greyed out until the checkbox is on.

The Inverse Foreground parameter, at the top of the Non-Uniform tab, is on by default. See Inverse non uniform in foreground.

Catseye

Catseye is an effect that cuts the bokeh by itself depending on the position. It is not visible in the center, but most visible in the edges.

Its parameters are Amount (0 to 2, slider up to 1, default 0.5), Gamma, Softness and Inverse.

Barndoors

Barndoors is an effect that occurs when light is blocked by a barndoor/flare. This is attached to the lens itself.

With OpenDefocus all sides can be controlled uniquely: Top, Bottom, Left and Right each have their own position.

Astigmatism

Bokehs can appear stretched at the corners, this is called Astigmatism in lens terms (note: eye astigmatism is something different).

Axial aberration

Axial aberration is affected by light rays not entering the lens the same way depending on the focus distance. Instead of regular chromatic aberration that shifts the edges of the image, this is on a bokeh level.

Focus on foreground and background, notice the shift from red on the outside to blue on the outside of the bokeh.

It is also possible to change the colour combination with the Colors parameter. All the options are:

  • Red / Blue (default)
  • Blue / Yellow
  • Green / Purple

Inverse non uniform in foreground

The Inverse Foreground parameter is on by default. This is optically accurate. This is a technical story so the explanation can be found on the Inverse foreground page. In simple terms it means the light rays inverse in the foreground with respect to the background.

What happens visually, is the following:

In the first image, everything is out of focus. In the second image the foreground is now in the out of focus, but in the foreground, and the background is in focus. Notice how the non uniform shapes across the edges of the image are inversed.

Detailed

The pages here are meant as an extensive documentation for users who like to know more about what a specific parameter is doing.

Features that are too detailed for the Quickstart guide, are documented here.

Check the sidebar for the topic you want to know more about.

Index

  • Bokeh
  • Camera
  • Depth
  • Size
  • Focal plane
  • Non uniform
    • Catseye
    • Barndoors
    • Astigmatism
    • Axial aberration

Bokeh

OpenDefocus has a bokeh creation tool built in, on the Bokeh tab. This allows you to easily create realistic looking bokeh shapes.

Filter type

The Filter Type parameter selects one of three shapes:

  1. Simple
  2. Disc
  3. Blade

Simple

The fast built-in filter. It is calculated on the fly, which costs a little less work. The other Bokeh parameters are ignored and greyed out.

Disc

This shape does not have any edges and is perfectly circular.

Blade

This shape allows you to set the number of corners with Blades (3 to 16 on the slider, default 5), and adjust its curvature with Curvature (default 0.5).

Shape parameters

With Disc or Blade selected, these parameters shape the bokeh:

  • Radius: the radius of the bokeh kernel.
  • Ring Color and Ring Size: the brightness and width of the bokeh ring.
  • Inner Color: the brightness of the bokeh interior.
  • Outer Blur and Inner Blur: the softness of the outer and inner bokeh edges.
  • Angle: the rotation of the bokeh shape.
  • Aspect Ratio: the squeeze of the bokeh shape.
  • Noise Size, Noise Intensity, Noise Octaves and Noise Seed: the noise applied to the bokeh shape.

Blades is only enabled for Blade.

Camera Input

OpenDefocus supports a camera for optically accurate defocus. This means that according to the camera settings the defocus would match the result from real life. Using the camera is easy.

There is no need for any position data. If you want your defocus to match for example a 50mm lens, set Focal Length and the filmback, and set the F-Stop to your expected stop.

Use Camera

Enable Use Camera on the Camera tab. The circle of confusion is then computed from the camera values: Focal Length (default 50 mm), F-Stop (default 16), Filmback Width and Height (default 24.576 x 18.672 mm). These rows have no effect while Use Camera is off, but they are not greyed out.

The camera applies in Depth mode with Math not set to Direct. In that case Camera Max Size (default 10) replaces Max Size.

Camera input

In hosts that provide the Nuke camera extension, the plugin has an optional Camera input. When a camera is connected, Use Camera is forced on, and the focal length and filmback come from the camera. F-Stop and World Unit still come from their parameters. This input is untested in Nuke.

World unit

It is important to set the World Unit the depth map was rendered in (mm, cm, dm, m, in or ft, default m). As each pixel in a depth map represents the distance, a value of 10 cm would be a completely different result as it would be if it is 1000 cm.

!!! warning annotate “Math” As with any depth based defocus, always make sure the Math is set correctly. Refer to Depth math for more information.

Controlling the focal plane

The Focal Plane parameter (Defocus tab) sets the depth that stays in focus. When the camera is used, the focal plane must be a positive distance, in the World Unit. See Focal plane.

I’d like to increase the size of the bokeh

Just as with any real camera, increase or decrease the f-stop to change the size of the bokeh. Lowering the F-Stop will make the bokeh larger, while increasing the F-Stop will decrease the bokeh size.

Make sure to set Camera Max Size to the size you want to define as a maximum, as no value will go higher than that.

Depth math

The Math parameter (Defocus tab) is important when using depth based defocus, and should be the first thing to set up correctly.

It defines what kind of depth map is given to the Depth input. There are three options: Real, 1/Z and Direct, which bypasses the depth conversion.

1/Z

This is a depth map stored as the reciprocal of the distance (1/Z). The brighter the value, the closer it is to the camera. These values are always between 0 and 1. This is the default.

Real

The value in a specific pixel is the distance in the scene’s World Unit (set on the Camera tab). This is the most common depth map.

Direct

With Math set to Direct, the Depth input is mapped directly to the size of the defocus in pixels. If you provide a value of 20 on a specific pixel, it will be defocussed by a size of 20.

It is also possible to define far and near field. Any negative value will be defined as in the far field, while any positive value will be defined as in the near field.

Important

While the direct map defines the size, it does not define the Max Size. Set your Max Size according to your expected or wished maximum value.

Defocus size

Besides the regular Size parameter there is also Max Size, and Size Multiplier which scales both. Understanding and using them properly can help get better results.

Note

This is only important for Depth mode. In 2D mode the defocus is Size times Size Multiplier.

With Use Camera on, Camera Max Size is used in place of Max Size.

Understanding Max Size

The regular Size parameter will set the size of the defocus in pixels when something is out of focus, in the far field.

Max Size controls two things simultaneously:

  1. The maximum sets the maximum defocus size across the entire image. This is a clamping value.
  2. The maximum defines the defocus size in the near field.

Example

If Size is set to 5, the defocus size in the far field will be 5. If Max Size is set to 5, it will also be 5 in the near field. Lowering this value would clamp the size for the entire image.

However, increasing Max Size, for example to 10, will result in a defocus size of 10 in the near field.

This is shown in this following video. First, Size and Max Size are set to the same value, resulting in the same defocus across the image. Then: Max Size is increased and shows that the near field also increases.

Limits

The effective radius is Max Size times Size Multiplier in Depth mode, and Size times Size Multiplier in 2D mode. Renders with an effective radius above 16384 px are rejected, and the error is written to the host’s log.

Focal plane

The Focal Plane parameter (Defocus tab) sets the depth that stays in focus. Depths in front of it fall in the near field, and depths behind it in the far field.

The Protect parameter sets the width of an in-focus band around the focal plane. The default is 0.

!!! note “Only for depth and camera based” In 2D mode the defocus is set by Size alone, so the focal plane has no effect.

Setting the focal plane

Set Focal Plane to the depth you want in focus. With Use Camera on, it must be a positive distance in the World Unit. The plugin has no focal plane preview, so the output always shows the defocus.

Non uniform

No lens is perfect, although lens manufactures get pretty close. While they try to make a lens as perfect as possible, in Visual Effects we try to replicate as many things possible. Including lens artifacts.

Replicating these things as close as possible to the real lenses makes an image from feeling CG to feeling authentic and real.

Example of image with lens artifacts replicated, especially notice the corners of the image. No single bokeh is the same across the image.

Non uniform is a broader term describing defocus artifacts that occur not at an image level, but on a per-bokeh basis. This mostly is dependant on these three factors:

  • xy position
  • focal distance
  • distance of point to camera

OpenDefocus included non uniform lens effects

OpenDefocus includes all kinds of non-uniform lens effects (artifacts) that you can replicate. All of these are documented here. Click the link to get more information about how these artifacts occur:

  • Catseye
  • Barndoors
  • Astigmatism
  • Axial aberration
  • Inverse foreground

Catseye

Catseye is a non uniform lens artifact that creates “cat-eyed” bokeh shapes at the edge of your image. Besides catseye, it is also called swirly bokeh, mechanical vignetting and optical vignetting.

Example image of effect

Take a closer look at the edges of the image. Compare the center bokeh to the edge and notice how it gets the cat-eyed shape.

Why do they occur?

This is a combination of the type of lens used and the angle of the light rays entering the lens. Best example is by looking at a real lens.

Imagine the point of the photo is a lightray entering the lens. Look at how the shape of the circle changes when looking at an angle at the lens. There is a portion of the lens which is not visible anymore.

Barndoors

Barndoors, or also called matte box, are physically attached doors to the lens, which allow the camera operator to block incoming light. This is often done to prevent lens flares from occurring.

Example of effect

Take a closer look at the edges of the image. Compare the center bokeh to the edge and notice how it gets the cat-eyed shape.

Why do they occur?

This is happening because the matte box/barndoors is attached to the lens and is blocking light from entering. It is identical to Catseye, but the shape is different because a matte box is straight and not in a circle shape like a lens.

Barndoors on lens attached

Best example is to look this image and notice how this would block light rays from entering the lens.

By Reinis Traidas - reinn - https://www.flickr.com/photos/reinis/398614200/, CC BY 2.0, https://commons.wikimedia.org/w/index.php?curid=4642883

Controlling each side individually

As you can see in the barndoors image, there could be four doors to control. This is top, bottom, left and right. With OpenDefocus each side has its own parameter: Top, Bottom, Left and Right (position, default 100). The Amount, Gamma and Inverse parameters apply to the whole effect, and are only enabled when Enable is on.

Astigmatism

Astigmatism is an artifact that creates stretched shapes gradually towards the edges of the image.

Note

Often confused with: it is a different phenomenon compared to what happens in human eyes with astigmatism. Which is causing lens flares.

Example image of effect

Why does it occur?

Astigmatism occurs when light rays from different directions not align at the same focal point in the lens. This causes the image to be distorted.

Axial aberration

Axial aberration, or also called longitudinal aberration is an artifact that occurs depending on the focus distance. It inverts the chromatic aberration depending on if it is in the near, or far field. Most often this is a combination of purple and green.

Example image of effect

Notice the aberration that changes depending on the near and far field.

Why does it occur?

Below is a video that shows the light ray entering the lens. But each light frequency is hitting the focal point at a different angle. This causes the chromatic aberration to change depending on the focus point. Also notice how the light rays inverse in the near field and the far field.

Tip

Near field is after the individual lines have collided (closer to the sensor), far field is before they have collided (closer to the lens).

What does the Amount parameter do?

It sets the angle light rays enters the lens:

Inverse foreground

The Inverse Foreground parameter (Non-Uniform tab) is on by default. It applies the non-uniform effects inverted for the foreground. This is caused by the way light rays work physically.

The Inverse checkbox on Catseye and Barndoors is a separate, per-effect option.

Visual explanation

When adjusting the focus, you are adjusting the position where lights intersect, and form a sharp image at that location.

The point where the lines collide is the point of focus. Notice that once they collided, they still continue in a straight line. This causes the light rays to be in an inverse order.

This is exactly the reason why optical artifacts inverse in the foreground in contrast to the far field: the light rays are in inversed order.

Example image

When Inverse Foreground is on, the shape is inversed in the foreground, in the opposite direction of the background:

Background and foreground in focus

Contributing to OpenDefocus

Contributing to OpenDefocus is greatly appreciated and welcome!

How work is organised

Work is driven by GitHub issues.

  1. Pick an open issue, or open one with the Task or Bug report template. A task describes the goal, context, scope, acceptance criteria and how to verify it, so anyone can pick it up. Comment on the issue before you start so work is not duplicated.
  2. Work on a branch named <type>/<issue>-<slug>, for example fix/142-roi-padding.
  3. Open a pull request against main that says Closes #<issue> and shows how each acceptance criterion was verified.

Report security vulnerabilities privately, as described in SECURITY.md.

Development

Working with coding agents

Agents start at AGENTS.md, which links to ARCHITECTURE.md, TESTING.md and SECURITY.md. Keep personal agent settings out of Git.

Tools Required

Install only the tools needed for your task:

TaskAdditional tools beyond stable Rust 1.95+ and native linker/build tools
Library, CLI or viewerNothing beyond stable Rust 1.95+ and native linker/build tools
Validate authored GPU shaderscargo xtask --compile --gpu uses Naga on stable Rust. Shader changes also need hardware correctness and performance checks. The excluded historical SPIR-V utility alone uses its pinned nightly.
Python tests and checksuv; each Python crate under crates/ manages its own environment.
Contributor pre-commit hooksRun uvx pre-commit run --all-files; the hook runs cargo xtask --precommit, which uses uvx for ty and validates shaders when kernel files change.
Build or serve documentationcargo install --locked cargo-about mdbook mdbook-toc
Browser viewerrustup target add wasm32-unknown-unknown, then cargo install --locked wasm-bindgen-cli --version 0.2.129; build with cargo xtask --compile --web.
OpenFX plugin bundleNothing beyond stable Rust 1.95+ and native linker/build tools; build with cargo xtask --bundle-ofx.

A normal CLI build is cargo xtask --compile --cli; a viewer build is cargo xtask --compile --ui. Neither requires Python tooling or nightly Rust. Avoid installing the documentation tools unless you work on documentation.

Checks before a pull request

Run cargo fmt --all -- --check and cargo xtask --test-crates for Rust changes. Use cargo xtask --compile --gpu for shader validation; hardware changes also need the correctness and performance checks in TESTING.md.

The imported Python crates in crates/ have their own environments, test commands, and CI.

Getting Started

  1. Fork the repository to your GitHub account.

    git clone git@github.com:YOUR_USERNAME/opendefocus.git
cd opendefocus/

  2. Create a branch for the issue, for example fix/142-roi-padding.

  3. Commit using the conventional commits specifications

  4. Run the development environment. Everything is handled through the xtask.

  5. Create your contribution and test the changes.

Most of all

Thank you!! For taking the time to read this and to contribute your time into this project.

Developer documentation

Thank you for considering to contribute to OpenDefocus! In this documentation you will find (hopefully) all you need to get started.

Structure

The project has multiple crates defined at the crates directory, each of them serve their own purpose:

Crate NameDescription
opendefocusThe actual public library itself. The main crate.

Supporting libraries

These independently versioned libraries are maintained in crates/ and serve applications beyond convolution:

Crate NameDescription
circle-of-confusionCamera and depth calculations
bokeh-creatorAperture generation
image-ndarrayImageBuffer and ndarray conversions

Crates

The project has multiple crates defined at the crates directory, each of them serve their own purpose:

  • opendefocus: The actual public library itself. The main crate.

opendefocus

This crate is the public rendering API. OpenDefocusRenderer initializes WGPU and renders scenes through one Adaptive GPU pipeline. Accurate integrates exact source-pixel weights; Fast uses its sample budget. Initialization and rendering failures return errors. WGPU selects the adapter, including its fallback adapter; there is no separate CPU or Legacy product path.

Source map

Paths below are relative to crates/opendefocus/src/:

PathResponsibility
lib.rsPublic renderer, supported-setting validation, full/stripe APIs, and data-model exports
error.rs, abort.rsRender errors and shared cancellation
runners/wgpu.rs, runners/adaptive_gpu.rsWGPU resources and shader dispatch
runners/adaptive.rsGPU buffer layouts, layers, pyramids, and diagnostics
kernel/shaders/Authored and embedded WGSL programs
kernel/stages/Shared host preparation and test-only algorithm references
worker/adaptive_render.rsFull-frame, ROI, stripe, and streamed rendering plans
renders/Aperture/image resizing and focal-plane preview helpers

The former opendefocus-kernel, opendefocus-shared and opendefocus-datastructure crates are now modules inside this crate. There is no CPU renderer; the Rust code only prepares host data and dispatches WGPU work.

Coordinates and image data

Rendering uses linear, premultiplied RGBA32F. Canonical prepared CoC is positive near and negative far. Direct-CoC input supplies signed pixel radii; real depth, 1/Z, and physical camera settings convert through the public data model. Nuke’s bundled fixture uses 1/Z, including focal zero and negative depth.

ROI and stripe calls retain full-frame coordinates and receive pristine padded source context. Use renderer.padding(settings) and keep the original color and depth immutable when assembling stripes. Astigmatic support can reach remote source chunks; limiting context to the destination region changes the result.

Validation

cargo xtask --compile --gpu checks all five WGSL sources with Naga on stable Rust. Shader syntax and ordinary unit tests do not establish hardware rendering quality. Renderer changes also need explicit ignored GPU tests, independent image/motion checks, and matched release benchmarks.

ARCHITECTURE.md records buffer and coordinate invariants.

Settings datamodel

The shared OpenDefocus settings are plain Rust structs and enums in src/datamodel/. mod.rs holds conversions and helper methods; model.rs holds the types. Messages nested in a message are modules (for example defocus::Settings or render::Filter). Enum fields are stored as i32 with typed getters and setters. Serde derives sit behind the serde feature. This used to be the separate opendefocus-datastructure crate.

xtask

Xtask coordinates development tasks across platforms. See building for the tools required by each task, and run cargo xtask --help for all options.

Things it covers

  • standalone evaluation UI: cargo xtask --compile --ui (builds the host executable in target/release)
  • browser viewer: cargo xtask --compile --web (static assets and an embedded HTML evaluation file in target/web)
  • standalone renderer CLI: cargo xtask --compile --cli
  • shader validation: cargo xtask --compile --gpu
  • testing: cargo xtask --test-crates
  • installer: cargo xtask --test-installer builds the OFX installer for this platform, installs it into a scratch folder, loads the installed plugin library and uninstalls it
  • render comparison: cargo xtask preview --head <cli> --base <cli> --out <folder> --image-base-url <url> renders the parity scenes and a focal-plane sweep of the 3D scene with two CLI builds and writes the images, the looping animations and the pull request comment
  • precommit checks cargo xtask --precommit
  • docs publishing
  • docs previewing: cargo xtask --serve-docs

Building

Build from the repository root with xtask. Normal builds require stable Rust 1.95+ and native linker/build tools. Cargo downloads the Rust dependencies.

cargo xtask --compile --cli
cargo xtask --compile --ui
cargo xtask --compile --gpu

The first two commands build the release CLI and native viewer. cargo xtask --bundle-ofx builds the OpenFX bundle OpenDefocus.ofx.bundle. The last validates the authored WGSL shaders with Naga. The renderer embeds WGSL directly; normal builds do not use nightly Rust or generated SPIR-V.

Rendering requires a WGPU adapter; initialization errors are returned.

Browser viewer

rustup target add wasm32-unknown-unknown
cargo install --locked wasm-bindgen-cli --version 0.2.129
cargo xtask --compile --web
python3 -m http.server --directory target/web 8080

Open http://localhost:8080/ in a WebGPU-enabled browser. target/web/ contains the HTML/JS/WASM hosting bundle and opendefocus-evaluation.html with the module embedded. HTTPS or localhost is required for the reliable browser preview. The documentation deployment workflow places the viewer under /demo/.

Checks

cargo fmt --all -- --check
cargo xtask --test-crates

Rust tests run the complete workspace in release mode with the locked dependency versions. Ignored hardware tests must be run explicitly for renderer changes (cargo test --release -p opendefocus --lib --locked -- --ignored --test-threads=1).

For local documentation, install cargo-about, mdbook, and mdbook-toc, then run cargo xtask --serve-docs. Use cargo xtask --help for the remaining tasks.

DEFINITIONS

Holder(s) under this licence and clearly marked as such. This may

as received under this licence.

or substituting -- in part or in whole -- any of the components of the
Original Version, by changing formats or by porting the Font Software to
a new environment.

copyright ownership of the Font Software.

additional naming elements to distinguish the Modified Version from the
Original Version. The name of such Modified Versions must be the name of
the Original Version, with "derivative X" where X represents the name of
the new work, appended to that name.

Font Software shall not be used to promote, endorse or advertise any
Modified Version, except (i) as required by this licence, (ii) to
